Title: Fortress: Fork Resistant Transparency logs Relying on Social graph Structure

  • Authors: Anastasiia Kucherenko, Melissa Chase, Esha Ghosh
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26327
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26327
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26327]] Fortress: Fork Resistant Transparency logs Relying on Social graph Structure(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26327)
  • Keywords: security
  • Abstract: Transparency systems -- such as Key Transparency (KT), Certificate Transparency, and binary transparency -- require that all honest participants see the same view of an untrusted server's directory, or, failing that, that the misbehaviour is publicly detected. Every existing design closes this gap with an external trust anchor: a publicly available bulletin board (BB), a blockchain, or an out-of-band (OOB) gossip network among auditors. We observe that transparency systems already have a built-in resource that has so far been overlooked: the social graph induced by the users themselves. We ask whether it can replace the bulletin board or the external gossip channel entirely, and answer in the affirmative (if occasional out-of-band interactions are allowed). We formalise a new primitive, Social-Graph based Forking-Resilient Transparency (sg-FORT), which captures the security guarantees of a BB-free transparency system and we construct Fortress Fork Resistant Transparency logs Relying on Social graph Structure, to our knowledge the first such protocol. In every epoch either all users output the same post, or at least one honest user produces a publicly verifiable Proof of Misbehaviour. Our guarantees hold against an arbitrary malicious server, including a snapshot adversary that may compromise a user and learn its secret state, as long as the server does not compromise close users. In honest executions, OOB interactions occur only when a user resets their secrets, so the total OOB cost approaches the natural lower bound of one call per reset -- dramatically below the full-gossip alternative. We prove security under standard cryptographic assumptions on signatures and dictionary commitment. Under simplifying assumptions (synchronous epochs, always-online users), we view this as a first feasibility result that opens up the design space of social-graph-based transparency systems.

However, safety-related information is often distributed across many directions in this space, causing downstream updates to overwrite parameters that encode safety behavior. We propose WSR-Tune, a Weight Space Reparameterization-based fine-tuning framework that preserves safety during downstream adaptation. By explicitly structuring the parameter space to disentangle safety and task information, our method reduces interference between objectives and enables simultaneous preservation of safety and improvement in downstream performance. Experimental results show that our method achieves a more favorable trade-off between downstream performance and safety retention, demonstrating its effectiveness for reliable LLM fine-tuning.

  • Abstract: This paper derives an exact attenuation formula for multivariate cross-moments under differential privacy achieved through randomized response. In particular, Diffusion Models have demonstrated superior image fidelity and sample diversity compared to other approaches on standard benchmarks, while also offering more flexible training dynamics as evidenced both in natural image domains and in medical applications. In medical imaging including Computed Tomography, Magnetic Resonance Imaging or Positron Emission Tomography, such models have various important use-cases including data augmentation, domain transfer, image reconstruction, inpainting, image augmentation and artifact correction. Further, generated images can be conditioned on patient characteristics, medical reports, segmentation masks or other images. Specific challenges arise due to the 3D volumetric nature of medical images, which translate to substantial demands in terms of memory, compute, and training data. This article provides a comparative overview of 3D Diffusion Models which have been developed for medical images, with a focus on current performance, limitations, and an outlook for future work.

Title: Wet-Biased Data Pipelines Mislead the Training and Evaluation of Generative Precipitation Nowcasting

  • Authors: Christoph Metzl, Stefan Kapsreiter, Kianusch Vahid Yousefnia, Virginia Poli, Richard Müller, Frank Krüger, Tobias Bölle
  • Subjects: cs.LG, physics.ao-ph
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26486
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26486
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26486]] Wet-Biased Data Pipelines Mislead the Training and Evaluation of Generative Precipitation Nowcasting(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26486)
  • Keywords: diffusion, generative
  • Abstract: Generative models for precipitation nowcasting are trained and evaluated predominantly on wet-biased datasets, which over-represent precipitation events to emphasize rare extremes. Selecting test cases based on target observations is known to favor forecasts that match the selected distribution rather than the intended deployment distribution. We show analytically how this target-dependent sampling also shifts the conditional distribution learned by generative models. When training and test sampling match, even an ideal model learns precisely the distribution favored by evaluation, allowing the evaluation bias to conceal the training bias. Using a fixed satellite-to-radar diffusion model, we demonstrate that target-dependent test sampling can reverse the calibration assessment. The model appears under-predictive on a wet-biased test set but over-predictive on a deployment-representative test set. This result is replicated outside the model's training domain after a seven-year gap. Utilizing classifier-free guidance as an example, we demonstrate how the contradictory calibration assessment can misdirect downstream model development.

Title: Mal-AAEGAN: Adversarial Autoencoder with Double-Critic WGAN-GP for Malware Generation

  • Authors: Nam Hoang Thanh, Trung Pham Duy, Lam Bui Thu
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26488
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26488
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26488]] Mal-AAEGAN: Adversarial Autoencoder with Double-Critic WGAN-GP for Malware Generation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26488)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Machine-learning (ML) malware detectors are known to be vulnerable to adversarial samples. Generative adversarial networks (GANs) such as MalGAN, Mal-LSGAN, and our previous Mal-D2GAN can craft adversarial malware to probe and harden these detectors, yet they operate directly on a high-dimensional, discrete binary feature space, which leads to unstable training, vanishing gradients, and adversarial samples of limited diversity. To address these problems we propose Mal-AAEGAN. First, an adversarial autoencoder (AAE) learns a low-dimensional, regularized and continuous latent representation of the $M$-dimensional binary API feature vector, and the generator performs an additive perturbation in this latent space rather than in the raw binary space. Second, training follows the Wasserstein GAN with gradient penalty (WGAN-GP) scheme using two critics: a mimic-black-box critic that inherits the role of the substitute detector and a naive critic that inherits the role of the additional detector. A decode-binarize-OR pipeline guarantees that the original API calls of the malware are preserved. The model is evaluated on two malware datasets of different input dimensionality ($M\in\{139,215\}$) against eight common ML detectors and compared, under an identical protocol, with MalGAN, Mal-LSGAN and Mal-D2GAN. Mal-AAEGAN trains stably in the continuous latent space and attains an evasion rate on par with or higher than these binary-space baselines across both datasets, while its generator stays responsive to the noise input rather than reducing to a single fixed perturbation per malware.

Title: ReAnchor: Reusing Rejected Verifier States for Diffusion Drafting

  • Authors: Sanghyun Lee, Wonjin Nam, Seungho Jang, Jaeho Lee, Jiho Park, Jaewoon Lee, Seungryong Kim

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26531

  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Block diffusion drafters accelerate speculative decoding by generating multiple draft tokens in a single forward pass. However, each pass produces only a fixed-length block from a single target-generated anchor, limiting how far the draft can extend. Meanwhile, target verification computes hidden states even at positions beyond the first rejection, but these states are typically discarded rather than reused. We propose ReAnchor, a training-free method that reanchors a pretrained drafter at shifted positions using discarded verifier states to generate parallel draft blocks. These blocks improve predictions within the original block and extend coverage beyond its endpoint. At sampling temperature of T=0, ReAnchor achieves its largest average gains on Gemma 4-12B, improving acceptance length by 19.4% and decoding speed by 17.9% across eight benchmarks. Averaged equally over four target models and eight benchmarks, it improves acceptance length by 15.2% and decoding speed by 13.4% with pretrained DFlash drafters.

  • Keywords: attack, robust
  • Abstract: Jailbreak methods are commonly compared using Attack Success Rate (ASR), but these comparisons depend on how responses are generated and judged. In this paper, we examine eight attacks against three open-weight language models using three evaluation procedures. Low-resource language attacks achieve a mean ASR of 96.0% under keyword matching but 2.7% under a Qwen3 evaluator following StrongREJECT scoring instructions. Title: BERT Is Already a Denoiser: Cheaply Unlocking Iterative Generation in Masked Language Models

  • Authors: Julio C. Amador Diaz Lopez
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26601
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26601
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26601]] BERT Is Already a Denoiser: Cheaply Unlocking Iterative Generation in Masked Language Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26601)
  • Keywords: diffusion, generative
  • Abstract: Masked language models (MLMs) such as BERT are typically used for representation learning despite their denoising pretraining. We test whether this capability supports conditional generation without diffusion training. Our decoder repeatedly predicts masked tokens and commits high-confidence predictions using a cosine schedule. It requires no diffusion timestep or reverse-diffusion objective. Frozen BERT already exhibits substantial denoising capability, reconstructing 61.0%, 37.5%, and 24.4% of masked tokens at 15%, 45%, and 60% corruption, respectively, although generation deteriorates sharply as visible context disappears. These results separate three components of masked iterative generation: pretraining supplies the representation and denoising operator, task adaptation determines what the model generates, and an iterative commitment rule supplies the decoding process. Explicit diffusion training is therefore not necessary to obtain effective iterative conditional generation from pretrained MLMs in the settings studied here.

  • Abstract: Invisible watermarks are used to mark who owns a painting. We show that a simple attack removes the most robust one we test. A simple probe that stores bits in the relative brightness of neighboring regions survives repainting, while a probe that uses absolute brightness does not. The same probe fails under diffusion regeneration, while VINE fails under repainting. Robustness to these two attacks is therefore a separate property, and benchmarks should test both.

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26685

  • Abstract: Rubric hacking limits both performance and training robustness in rubric-based reinforcement learning. From the sub-dimension perspective, we observe two phenomena: (1) dimension imbalance, causing the policy to favor heavily covered dimensions; (2) dimension absence, leading to a systematic gap between rewards and true quality.   • Abstract: Bitcoin-style Proof-of-Work (PoW) secures permissionless blockchains but spends substantial computation on hash puzzles which have no utility other than extending the chain. Title: Deep Pontryagin Learning for Continuous-Time Stochastic Control with Jumps

  • Authors: Jean-Loup Dupret, Patrick Cheridito
  • Subjects: cs.LG, eess.SY, math.OC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26718
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26718

  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: We develop a deep Pontryagin learning framework for high-dimensional continuous-time stochastic control problems that naturally accommodates jump-diffusions. In contrast to direct policy optimization, which repeatedly differentiates the objective through the entire time-discretized dynamics, our method separates adjoint learning from policy improvement by explicitly incorporating state-sensitivity information from the Pontryagin stochastic maximum principle. For a fixed Markov control, we first compute pathwise adjoint labels by backpropagating the reward through time along simulated state trajectories, and project these labels onto the Markov state through a supervised regression step. Numerical experiments illustrate the accuracy, stability, and scalability of the approach.

Title: Copying Under Repetition: From Prefix Matching to Position Hashing

  • Authors: Anxin Guo, Tianlong Huang, Chenxiao Yang, Zhiyuan Li
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26722
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26722
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26722]] Copying Under Repetition: From Prefix Matching to Position Hashing(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26722)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Frontier LLMs can copy random text almost flawlessly, yet they often overrun or truncate long blocks of repeated characters. In repeated strings, matching local content no longer reliably identifies where copying should continue or when it should stop. Nevertheless, we show that a two-layer transformer with $O(\log\log N)$-bit precision and $O(\log N/\log\log N)$ width can copy every string up to a chosen design horizon $N$ and terminate correctly under arbitrary repetition. We use RoPE-style rotations of attention values and outputs (VO-rotations) with small prime periods to encode distances from delimiter anchors modulo those primes. By the Chinese Remainder Theorem, these periodic encodings jointly distinguish all required distances, allowing one retrieval rule to select either the next source token or the end marker. In contrast, we prove a width-independent impossibility result for copying under sequential finite-precision softmax accumulation. It applies to transformers whose only positional input is an additive attention bias that eventually becomes constant, including NoPE and a recursive implementation of ALiBi. Once an identical-token run exceeds the resulting saturation threshold, further repetitions leave the logits unchanged. If this threshold grows sublinearly with $N$, these models cannot both complete a long all-identical copy and stop correctly, a pattern resembling the termination errors of frontier LLMs. In synthetic copying experiments, baseline transformer models fail on long repeated strings even within the training range. Trained VO models, by contrast, generalize beyond the training lengths with near-perfect accuracy up to length $\approx 5005$, the selected primes' period product.

  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26740
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26740
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26740]] CheckDistill: Adaptive Checklist Distillation for Efficient Distributional Reward Modeling in Image Editing(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26740)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Reinforcement learning for instruction-guided image editing requires reward models that provide reliable, accurate, and efficient feedback. We introduce CheckDistill, a pointwise reward modeling framework that internalizes instruction-adaptive checklists. The deployed student directly predicts four score distributions without checklist inputs or explicit reasoning and aggregates their expectations into a scalar reward. GMR employs a learned residual gate at scalar, low-rank, or per-dimension resolution to control how much of each cycle's proposed update is accepted into the state. This unifies halting and gating into a single adaptive computation objective while recovering PonderNet-style halting as a special case without gates. Layered on top of recAMPLIFY, GMR further reduces expected layer depth by up to 37%, although the accuracy trade-off varies by task. Title: Trajectory Replay: Continuous-Time Mixture Transport for Lifelong Generative Models

  • Authors: Alessandro Palma, Charles Jones, Tristan Cinquin, Fabian J. Theis, Frederik Wenkel
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26749
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26749
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26749]] Trajectory Replay: Continuous-Time Mixture Transport for Lifelong Generative Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26749)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Adapting pre-trained continuous-time generative models to sequentially incoming data without forgetting earlier tasks is a central challenge in lifelong learning. A leading strategy, generative replay, uses a generative model trained on historical tasks to augment its own continual update with synthetic samples from experience. However, it requires full trajectory simulations at every training step, which accumulate discretization errors and can degrade the replayed historical distribution. Here, we propose trajectory replay, which bypasses sample generation by training an updated velocity field to match both a frozen historical velocity along its trajectories and the incoming task's velocity field, weighted by a stability-plasticity coefficient. Through the lens of continuous-time transport, we show the resulting optimum averages the two task velocities by their respective densities and generates the mixture of their probability paths. Combined with mean velocity distillation, trajectory replay accesses historical trajectories using only a few network evaluations per continual update. Empirically, it outperforms generative replay and distillation baselines on class-incremental image generation. In two biological case studies, trajectory replay extends generative perturbation models to new contexts and experimental batches across two data modalities while preserving treatment-effect prediction.

Title: Topological Sheaves of Irreducibility: A Theoretical Framework for Multi-Agent Task Obstructions

  • Authors: Steven Motta, Davide Mambelli, Alessandro Rozza
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.MA
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26777
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26777
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26777]] Topological Sheaves of Irreducibility: A Theoretical Framework for Multi-Agent Task Obstructions(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26777)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Multi-agent systems can differ under identical agents and communication budgets when they support different forms of joint interaction. We introduce Topological Sheaves of Irreducibility (TSI), a sheaf-theoretic framework separating task-level compatibility obstructions from system capability. A task is represented by an interaction complex $K$ and a capacity sheaf $\mathcal{F}$; nonzero $H^n(K;\mathcal{F})$ defines a structural signature, while registered interventions test capability. For cocycle data, cochain realization is solvable exactly when the represented cohomology class vanishes. Examples show that the signature is sheaf-relative, and the degree-1 reduction yields cycle-space witnesses and observation requirements. We evaluate two finite one-round models. Across 15,625 degree-1 inputs, an all-agent additive primitive solves decision and repair exactly; the optimal pairwise system reaches 88.80% on decision, one input above the 88.79% majority-class baseline, and 26.63% on repair. On a degree-2 tetrahedral-boundary task over 531,441 inputs, the same-2-skeleton system retains all four three-agent primitives yet matches the pairwise-only optimum throughout the registered separation window, whereas adding the class-filling four-agent primitive makes both tasks exact. Status-preserving robustness checks retain exact performance but are not cost-matched to the class-restoring ablation. Conditional on the registered primitives and cost models, TSI provides task-relative, falsifiable tests of higher-order interaction.

Title: Accountable partial understanding: securing the epistemic attack surface of agentic systems

  • Authors: Josiah Dykstra
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26799
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26799

  • Keywords: security, defense, attack
  • Abstract: Attackers profit from what defenders do not understand. Software security has treated the gap between system behavior and human comprehension as a condition to narrow through analysis and assurance, booking the remainder as accepted risk. Agentic AI systems change that gap: when software reads, writes, explains, and authorizes security-relevant behavior, partial understanding moves faster, grows more opaque, and couples to delegated authority. This paper characterizes the resulting epistemic attack surface and presents a taxonomy of four interacting attack categories, which target the interpretation of intent, the production and consumption of evidence, the scope of delegated authority, and the comprehension of human supervisors. From the conditions, the paper derives five design principles: bounded delegation, decoupled evidence, assumption tracking with invalidation, role-specific accountability views, and intervention-preserving design with residual unknown registers. It introduces two measurable probe dimensions, evidence linkage and invalidation latency, for testing whether accountability mechanisms hold under change and adversarial pressure. Three running examples (an AI coding agent, an autonomous cyber-defense agent, and an AI-mediated insulin delivery update) ground the analysis. Security without full understanding is already here; the task is to make it accountable.

This tension suggests that safety and general reasoning should not be treated as separate training goals. We instead view safety as an LLM capability that can be learned through a reasoning structure shared with general tasks. They also retain safety under general-only GRPO without safety rewards, while subsequent RL reaches a better safety-utility frontier when initialized from the mid-trained models. Together, these results suggest that learning safety and general reasoning within a shared mid-training stage can reduce the safety-utility trade-off and make safety more robust to subsequent training.

Title: ASTOR: Benchmarking and Improving Autoformulation for Soft-Constraint Optimization with a Formal Grammar

This has given rise to autoformulation, which focuses on using LLMs to translate natural-language descriptions of decision problems into executable mathematical optimization models. We also introduce SoftOR, a unified benchmark of natural language problems which focuses on soft constraint formulations.   • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26837
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26837
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26837]] SpecKCast: Spectral Kalman Forecasting for Diffusion Acceleration(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26837)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) achieve high-fidelity image and video generation, but they suffer from substantial inference costs due to repeated transformer evaluations across many denoising steps. Feature caching reduces this cost by reusing features across denoising steps. Recent forecasting methods extend this idea by extrapolating features to skipped steps instead of reusing them. However, existing forecasting methods extrapolate the features they predict uniformly, even though the spatial-frequency components of these features change most at different stages of denoising. They also overlook that denoising progresses nonuniformly, so equal step intervals can correspond to very different amounts of change. These mismatches amplify forecast errors as more steps are skipped, limiting the achievable acceleration. We propose SpecKCast, a training-free method that forecasts the model output in the frequency domain. SpecKCast applies a 2D discrete cosine transform (DCT) to the output and tracks each coefficient with a lightweight Kalman filter along a band-specific time coordinate derived from the noise schedule. The filter tracks each coefficient's value and rate of change, and the transformer is evaluated only when the filter's forecast uncertainty exceeds a threshold. Experiments on FLUX.1-dev, Qwen-Image, Wan2.1-14B, and HunyuanVideo show that SpecKCast achieves a better trade-off between acceleration and fidelity than existing caching and forecasting methods, reaching up to 6x speedup with less than 0.05 GiB of additional memory.

Title: LatComm: Reusable Context Compression for Latent Communication between Large Language Models

  • Authors: Huawei Lin, Jinting Liu, Tan Yu, Weijie Zhao
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26840
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26840
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26840]] LatComm: Reusable Context Compression for Latent Communication between Large Language Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26840)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Systems that combine several large language models (LLMs) often use one context for many questions. When the context is passed between models as text, the receiving model processes all of its tokens again for every question. Avoiding this cost requires a context representation that is compact, reusable across questions, and readable by a different LLM. Existing compression and latent communication methods each provide only some of these properties. We introduce LatComm, a Latent Communication interface that provides all three by separating encoding from reading: the context is encoded once without the question, and the receiver selects what to read for each question. A frozen context encoder LLM compresses the context into a fixed-size packet and sends it to a different frozen receiver LLM, which reads the packet through cross-attention, with gates computed from its prompt. On 12 multiple-choice question-answering datasets, LatComm improves average accuracy over single-vector baselines by up to 10.2 points. On QuALITY, it reduces the receiver's input by 57x and answers the questions of an article 3x faster.

Title: VideoMaMa++: Temporally Consistent Video Matting via Preserve-and-Refine Token

  • Authors: Sangbeom Lim, Seoung Wug Oh, Heeji Yoon, Seungryong Kim, Joon-Young Lee
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26857
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26857
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26857]] VideoMaMa++: Temporally Consistent Video Matting via Preserve-and-Refine Token(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26857)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Diffusion-based video matting has recently emerged as a leading approach, leveraging video diffusion priors from internet-scale pretraining to achieve strong zero-shot generalization from limited synthetic data. However, the heavy compute of these diffusion backbones limits inference to a short chunk of just 6 to 24 frames, so even ordinary videos span multiple chunks. Because each chunk is matted independently from coarse mask guidance, existing methods fail to maintain consistency across chunk boundaries, producing flickering edges and discontinuous transparency. We address this with a mask-guided diffusion-matting framework built on two ideas. The RGB and mask conditioning are represented as separate token streams that interact through full spatio-temporal self-attention, letting the model adaptively combine fine RGB texture with coarse mask layout rather than committing to a fixed channel-wise fusion. On top of this, we propose a novel pair of learnable embeddings, the preserve token and the refine token, that act as a per-frame conditioning interface and enable a sliding-window inference scheme in which already-generated mattes are propagated across chunk boundaries for local temporal consistency, complemented by a globally shared reference anchor at every chunk to prevent error accumulation in long sequences without any additional training. Across standard video matting benchmarks and in-the-wild long footage, our framework outperforms prior diffusion-based methods in both fine-detail accuracy and temporal consistency, and uniquely maintains consistency across chunk boundaries on long videos.

Title: OpenVLHarness: Real World Visual Agentic Tool-Use with Multimodal Memory

  • Authors: Yu Zhou, Cheng-Fu Yang, Rui Sun, Di Wu, Sibo Peng, Mingyang Zhang, Zhen Yang, Ruiyi Zhang, Nanyun Peng, Zhe Gan, Kai-Wei Chang
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.MM
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26860
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26860
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26860]] OpenVLHarness: Real World Visual Agentic Tool-Use with Multimodal Memory(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26860)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made rapid progress on visual reasoning, yet many tasks require evidence that is difficult to obtain through internal reasoning alone: our controlled study on 22 benchmarks shows that simply increasing the inference budget of the base model yields only modest gains. Such tasks demand grounded perception, external knowledge, and numerical measurements obtained through heterogeneous tools, whose outputs must be made interpretable to the model and retained across reasoning steps. We introduce OpenVLHarness, a multimodal reasoning harness organized around a capability layer and persistent multimodal memory. The capability layer combines task-level tool interfaces with evidence rendering, providing consistent access to heterogeneous backends and converting their outputs into model-readable observations. Persistent multimodal memory retains the corresponding images, structured data, and web references as addressable artifacts throughout each query. A coding interface executes generated programs over stored data, enabling computations that combine results from multiple tools. Together, these mechanisms support an iterative process of acquiring, inspecting, and composing multimodal evidence. Evaluated on 22 benchmarks with eight open and proprietary backbones, OpenVLHarness improves average scores by 9.7-12.6 points over matched no-tool baselines, including +11.2 with Qwen3-VL-8B, +9.7 with Kimi K3, and +9.9 with GPT-6 Astra. With GPT-6 Luna at medium reasoning effort, OpenVLHarness outperforms the base model, Codex, and Visual Sketchpad at maximum effort by 7.7-8.5 points while incurring at most 37% of their model-token cost.

Title: Text-to-Image Generative Modeling Without Language Pretraining

  • Authors: Keshigeyan Chandrasegaran, Yue Zhao, Armin W. Thomas, Ehsan Adeli, Jiajun Wu, Juan Carlos Niebles, Li Fei-Fei, Michael Poli
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26863
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26863
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.26863]] Text-to-Image Generative Modeling Without Language Pretraining(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26863)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Modern text-to-image (T2I) systems use internet-scale language pretraining for text-conditioned image generation. We ask whether language pretraining is necessary for strong T2I generation. We introduce Halcyon, a family of early-fusion multimodal autoregressive models pretrained entirely from scratch on GPIC, a dataset of 100M image-text pairs. Halcyon-0.52B outperforms LlamaGen-XL/8 (48.0 vs. 63.3 gFD) with 18.2$\times$ less pretraining compute. Further, Halcyon-1.6B outperforms JiT-T2I-XXL/8++ (35.3 vs. 41.0 gFD) with 275$\times$ less pretraining compute. Controlled experiments show that text-only pretraining does not improve T2I generation. Halcyon also learns image captioning by replacing caption dropout with I2T supervision, maintaining competitive T2I generation quality under the same pretraining budget. We show that strong T2I systems can be trained from scratch without internet-scale language pretraining. Code and models are available at this https URL
